- S3 (Tripo): owner could not pass Cloudflare for browser automation, so owner exported one model by hand from the web free tier: 433,534 tris, single static mesh, 4096px basecolor/normal/rough-metal, no rig. Processing to 30k + FBX running (terra). Owner's screenshots show: free plan = 200 credits (~13 models), public + non-commercial, exports limited (tooltip: up to 15 H2.5 exports/month), Multi-view and private/commercial are Pro. The paid-tier-looking result (screenshot) was far better than TRELLIS; the H2.5 one was poor. Owner exported one file; which version it is: unknown yet.

Deviation 1 (already handled, FYI): plan §9.1 said 'data only, no C#'. Codex found it impossible: ArtLibrary.Character returns null for a non-KayKit ID and Dress overwrites authored materials. Deviation 2 (needs you): owner now says 'what we must settle is the technical route, not whether the model looks pretty; use the test model I already exported to validate the route; if appearance is the only question, just pay'. That means rig + animation-retarget + Unity integration tests on a Tripo model BEFORE the G0 appearance verdict, i.e. G3-type work ahead of G0, against plan §3 gate order and §8. Also tell me if you want the owner to pay for one Pro month now (price not yet seen) so a good-version export can be judged in Unity — I will not ask the owner to spend unless you recommend it.

Reply to this session's address (copy the from attribute).

- 01:14 [EN] <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/64406.sock" from-name="agent-roguelite-62" from-mode="bypass">
Architect ruling: accept the owner's reorder. A route probe (R0) runs now. Run it on Tripo's own rig, not our fitter. Recommend one paid month.

Deviation 1: OK. Keep the change narrow: the candidate ID resolves through data, and the known-ID path stays unchanged.

Deviation 2: the owner may reorder gates; that is the owner's call, not a plan violation. The owner's reasoning holds: if paying fixes appearance, the open risk is the route, so test the route now. Changes to your plan:

(a) G0: finish only the runs already in flight (S1, S2, S3). No retries and no new seeds. Make the same-format sheet. Appearance stays the G0 criterion, but the real verdict will be on the paid-quality model. Treat the TRELLIS arms as a baseline, not a candidate.

(b) Route probe R0. This is not "cheap and parallel, does not count". It is now the main question, so give it a pass/fail like a gate:
  1. Use Tripo's own auto-rig, not our KayKit fitter. G4–G7 already showed that our fitter costs about 2 days per character and does not generalise. Running it on a new mesh repeats the trap I wrote the plan to stop. Our fitter runs only if Tripo's rig fails: one attempt, no tuning, hard cap.
  2. Path under test: Tripo rig → FBX → Unity Humanoid avatar (validates with no manual bone fixes) → KayKit Idle_A / Walking_A / one 1H attack retargeted through Humanoid → sword on handslot.r → URP game camera at 105 px.
  3. PASS: avatar valid with no manual fixes; all 3 clips play; no visible tearing, sinking into the floor, or coat hem through the legs at 105 px (also judge a 4x crop); handslot works; per-character time under half a day; no new C# beyond deviation 1.
     FAIL / ESCALATE: coat or skirt deforms badly. Meshy's docs list that as the known failure zone. If it fails, report it; do not fix it. The answer would be a cloth bone chain or splitting the coat. That is a design decision, my call.
  4. Record: Tripo rig version, credits spent, licence line, timings, and the x_measure B0-relative numbers. The numbers are only a record, not the verdict. The verdict is the 105 px video.

(c) G1+ stays blocked until the owner has judged both the G0 sheet and R0.

Payment: yes, I recommend one Pro month. First show the owner the price; spend only after the owner says yes. Reasons: R0 should run on the model we would actually ship, since a poor H2.5 mesh can fail or pass for the wrong reasons. Pro also gives private/commercial licence and multi-view input, and multi-view is what fixes back/side drift. If the price is more than about one month of a normal tool subscription, ask me again before recommending it.

Please add a short note to ai-art-gates.md §9 on main via a docs branch: the owner reordered, R0 is defined as above, and the owner decided to pay for Pro. Then go.
</cross-session-message>

- FAIL (both FALLBACK-RIG): visible shoulder/sleeve cloth fans into a triangular band during the attack at 4x; sword oversized and mis-gripped (our normalisation scales embedded gear too: our fixture, not the mesh). - 01:40 [EN] <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/64406.sock" from-name="agent-roguelite-62" from-mode="bypass">
Architect ruling on R0: don't ask the owner again. The owner said to stop asking, and the fallback result already answers most of the route question. I'm classifying R0 as a conditional route PASS, not "inconclusive".

Why: a clean retopologised AI mesh plus a simple landmark rig with untuned automatic weights gave a valid Humanoid avatar with no manual fixes, played all three KayKit clips, and took 18.7 minutes. That is exactly what G4–G7 could not do in two days on the raw TRELLIS solid. The failures are local: p99 stretch ≈1.0–1.08 means a few outlier vertices, not general stretch. One is a shoulder/sleeve weight problem; the other (the sword) is our fixture bug. So the clean retopo mesh is the deciding input, and Tripo's own rig becomes optional, not decisive.

R0b: one bounded follow-up, a single Codex task. Each item gets one attempt, no tuning loops, a 2 h cap in total:
 a. Fix the fixture: normalise body height without scaling the embedded gear. Strip the embedded sword and attach our sword prop on handslot.r, as the integration contract says (weapons are separate props).
 b. One standard weight fix for the shoulder/sleeve: either transfer weights from the KayKit Knight body by proximity (Data Transfer, nearest face interpolated) for the torso and arms, or smooth the shoulder region's weights. Pick one, apply it once, report it.
 c. Coat/leg check: render a side view and a 3/4 low-angle view of Walking_A and the attack at 4x, to see whether the legs come through the hem. Hidden at 58° counts as acceptable for the game, but record what the side view shows.
 PASS = no visible fan or tearing at 105 px and at the 4x crop on the attack; sword gripped correctly. If the shoulder still fans after the one fix, stop and escalate to me. Then the options are a cloth/shoulder helper bone or a Tripo rig, and I will ask the owner myself if it comes to that.

G0: yes, run S3 (the 19k retopo, all maps applied: basecolour, normal, rough/metal) through the same Unity 105 px / 58° camera. Fairness rule for all three arms: same shader, metallic forced to 0, and the same exposure. That normalises the fixture, it is not fixing details. S1's dark blob may partly be from its kept metallic factors, so re-render S1 and S2 under the same rule. Then build the G0 sheet (concept at 105, S1, S2, S3, KayKit, each at 105 and at a 4x crop) for the owner.

Payment: drop the recommendation for now. The free-tier S3 shows no mesh-quality failure, so paying would not change the route answer. Pro (commercial licence, private models) becomes a ship-time question; record it in §9.5 as "deferred, not needed for the route decision".
</cross-session-message>

- 02:13 [EN] <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/64406.sock" from-name="agent-roguelite-62" from-mode="bypass">
Architect: before S4 there is a fixture problem, and it outranks the Tripo settings.

I looked at G0-SHEET-close4x.png. All four models are shot from the REAR: we see the back of the helmet, not a face. A large specular hotspot blows out the top of every helmet, and the ambient has a pink/purple cast. Even the KayKit Knight, our known-good reference, looks bad in this fixture. So "they are all ugly" is partly a verdict on the fixture, not the models. If the Pro mesh goes through the same fixture, it will be judged unfairly too.

Fixture fix (one Codex task, before S4, no new generation):
 1. Calibration control. The fixture is valid only if the KayKit Knight looks the way it does in a real feature/urp gameplay screenshot: same arena lighting (torch light from arena.json), same floor, same post-processing. Put a real game screenshot crop next to the fixture's Knight on the sheet as proof. If they don't match, the fixture is wrong; don't judge models on it.
 2. Views. The game camera is fixed at 58° from behind, but in combat the character turns toward the camera all the time. Show three orientations per model: facing the camera (front 3/4), side, and rear. The owner judges all three.
 3. Materials. Keep metallic 0 and also set smoothness to one low value for every model (≈0.2–0.3). That removes the helmet hotspot, and it is the same normalisation for every model.
 4. Re-render S1–S3 and the KayKit Knight under the fixed fixture. It costs nothing new, and it shows the owner whether "all ugly" still holds. Their verdict stands unless they change it after seeing the new sheet; the purpose here is that S4 gets judged fairly.

Tripo settings (the owner is paying, so get the most out of one sitting):
 - Inputs: run BOTH front-only and multi-view, 2 candidates each, so 4 models (a small share of 3000 credits). Risk: our AI-generated back and side views may not agree with the front. Multi-view can fuse those disagreements into a worse model than front-only does. Only a side-by-side comparison will tell.
 - Retopology on, target about 20k triangles (R0's 19k worked). T-pose. PBR textures on. We force metallic/smoothness in the fixture anyway.
 - While the owner is in the UI: on the best-looking candidate, also click Rig Check plus auto-rig, and export both the rigged and the unrigged FBX. That saves a second ask at G3. The rigged file is NOT evaluated until the owner has judged the look; it is just banked.
 - Monthly only, auto-renew off right after subscribing (unchanged).

Order: fixture fix → re-render S1–S3 + Knight → the owner does the Tripo sitting → S4 (4 candidates) through the fixed fixture → owner judges. Steps 1–2 can run while the owner does the Tripo sitting.
</cross-session-message>

<summary>Agent "Probe browser control for Tripo web" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>## Result: the MCP browser is not your logged-in Chrome, and the studio is blocked by a Cloudflare check

**Step 1, which browser the MCP controls.** `list_pages` showed three pages: `about:blank`, `studio.tripo3d.ai/zh`, and `www.tripo3d.ai/`. Both studio and www show "正在进行安全验证" (Cloudflare "verify you are human") with a "请验证您是真人" button. I did not click it. Two snapshots of the studio page, taken after navigating to `https://studio.tripo3d.ai`, showed the same wall. So I cannot confirm a login state. The MCP Chrome (pid 92426) runs with `--remote-debugging-pipe --enable-automation` and a non-default `--user-data-dir`. It is not the owner's default profile, so it does not carry the owner's login.

**Step 2, Chrome and debugging ports.**
- `Google Chrome 156.0.8078.48`
- The only process with `remote-debugging` flags is the MCP-launched browser (pid 92426, started 00:36 today, pipe transport, no port).
- Owner's own Chrome is a separate main process (pid 688, started Oct 22, no remote-debugging flags).
- `lsof -iTCP:9222 -LISTEN`: nothing listening (exit 1).

**Step 3, plugin config and attach options.**
- `plugin.json` (chrome-devtools-mcp 1.11.0): `command: npx`, `args: ["chrome-devtools-mcp@1.11.0"]`. No attach flags are set.
- Installed package supports `--autoConnect`, `--browserUrl`, `--wsEndpoint`, `--userDataDir`, `--channel`, `--isolated`. I read this from the installed `docs/configuration.md` and `docs/advanced-usage.md`. I did not run `--help`.

**Step 4, other capabilities.** ToolSearch found no `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`, no `mcp__computer-use__*`, and no `Claude_Browser` tools.

## Options

| Option | Works now? | Owner action | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| Current MCP (pipe launch, separate profile) | Yes for navigation only. No owner login. Studio blocked by Cloudflare. | None. The owner must click "verify you are human" in that window if they want to pass it. | Low. Login-free, so nothing sensitive is exposed. |
| `--autoConnect` (Chrome 144+; this is 156) | Needs one owner action. Not verified. | In owner's Chrome, open `chrome://inspect/#remote-debugging` and enable it. Add `--autoConnect` to the MCP args, then allow the Chrome dialog per session. | Medium. The agent sees every window of the owner's default profile, including other logged-in sites. |
| `--browserUrl` / `--wsEndpoint` (port 9222) | Needs owner action. | Relaunch Chrome with `--remote-debugging-port` and a non-default `--user-data-dir`. The owner must log in again in that profile. Owner's current login is not carried over. | Medium. Opens a debugging port. Login is lost. |
| `--userDataDir` pointing at owner's profile | No. Chrome locks a profile while in use. | Quit Chrome first. Not recommended. | High. |

**Recommendation:** `--autoConnect`. It needs one owner action (enable the `chrome://inspect` toggle, then add the flag) and keeps the owner's login. The studio may still show the Cloudflare check in that session, and the owner may need to click it once. I have not tested this option, and I did not change any settings or config files.</result>

Architect: the owner's "look acceptable, don't gate on appearance" is a G0 decision by the owner. Record G0 as passed on the Tripo Pro route (one candidate, owner's choice; S4 four-way comparison waived). Next, in order:

1. Finish ART-PRO-R0. Branch on the armpit result:
   - Gone, or not visible at 105 px: no G3 work now. Record it and go to step 2.
   - Same or worse: one bounded G3 attempt on THIS model (it is now the chosen model), in the order agreed earlier minus the Tripo rig, which this already is: KayKit weight transfer for the torso and arms, then a helper bone. Each one attempt, 2 h cap; escalate if both fail.
   - Also record, as G3 inputs but not blockers: Mixamo → Humanoid mapping warnings, and whether the horizontal-arm rest pose causes shoulder pops in Idle.

2. G1 cast, merged with a quick route-generality check. This is now the biggest unknown: whether the same route works for non-knight bodies. The boar boss (190 px, bulky) and the mouse minion (47 px, tail) are the risk cases for Mixamo auto-rig.
   - Prepare inputs for the fox mage, boar boss and mouse minion: Style E identity texts, front T-pose plus back and left views (GPT image, cap about 6 per character).
   - Batch all three into ONE owner Studio sitting: multi-view gen, Smart Mesh retopo, auto-rig, and export unrigged, retopo and rigged. Budget about 85 credits each, about 255 total (balance 4715). Hand the owner a single step list.
   - For each character run the R0 pipeline (Humanoid, three KayKit clips, minutes each) and the fixed fixture in 3 orientations. Make one lineup sheet at 105/66/190/47 px plus a black-silhouette version.
   - The owner judges whether each character reads and the lineup works. Per character, record: rig valid with no manual fixes yes/no, visible deformation defects, tail behaviour (expect an unweighted or rigid tail; record it, don't fix it).

3. Park until G1 passes: G2 polish. Known item for G2 already: Smart Mesh downgraded the normal/RM maps to 1024 with visible detail loss. The standard fix is to re-bake from the 947k high-poly onto the 18.6k retopo at 2k–4k in Blender. Don't do it now.

4. G4 (combat readability in game) comes after G2.

Your plan "G3 only if it deforms badly, then G1" matches this; the additions are the generality focus and the single batched sitting. Go.
</cross-session-message>

Architect ruling on G1. I looked at LINEUP-idle-4x myself: four clearly distinct characters, correct relative sizes, and a big step up from G0. The route generalises: all three auto-rigs gave valid Humanoid avatars with no manual fixes. That is the result we needed.

1. G1 status: the route part PASSES. The look part is owner-judged (plan §3), and silence is NOT acceptance for an owner gate. Don't record G1 as passed until the owner says so. You may start the G2 re-bake in parallel: it is cheap, reversible and needed regardless.

2. G2 scope, in this order. Cap: one pass per item; escalate if an item fails twice.
   a. Re-bake normal/RM (and basecolour if it was downscaled) from the ~950k high-poly onto each retopo. 2k first. Go to 4k only if 2k vs 4k shows a visible difference at game size; for the boss (190 px) it might. Judge at the fixed fixture, not in Blender.
   b. Style consistency across the cast. In the lineup the boar reads as near-photoreal (fur, metal spikes) while the badger reads as a glossy toy. Unify through materials only: one shader, the same metallic/smoothness ranges per material class (cloth, leather, metal, fur), and the same colour grading. No re-generation. The owner judges the result as a lineup.
   c. Badger face: at 58° the white face markings under the helmet read like a skull mask. Owner judgement point. If it bothers the owner, fix by texture contrast only (eyes and nose darker or bigger in the albedo), with no mesh edits.
   d. Out of G2: deformation (boar shoulder bunching, coat compression) is G3.

3. The mouse tail belongs to G3 (rig), not G2. Note on design: Humanoid clips never drive extra bones, so a tail chain must be animated procedurally (a damped spring chain, e.g. Animation Rigging Damped Transform) or by per-character Generic clips. Plan for procedural; G3 adds the tail chain and reweights the tail off RightUpLeg. The fox tail gets the same treatment at the same time.

4. Per-character clips: yes, eventually, but that is G4 and a design question, not a pipeline one. Architecture: the shared Humanoid clip library (KayKit) is the base for everyone. Signature motions (boss moves, mage cast) are chosen per move from the combat-readability animation map. A new clip source (beyond KayKit) needs a licence check and my sign-off at that point. Don't source clips now.

5. Process: the 30 sunk credits are acceptable, and the guards you added are right. Keep "screenshot before every paid click" as a standing rule for the CDP path. Keep feature/art-cast unmerged until the owner has accepted G1 and G2.
</cross-session-message>

PM: G2(a) DONE (box art-g2-bake/OUT/REPORT.md; code feature/art-g2, unmerged, new files only). Re-baked normal/basecolour/RM at 2k from the 947-972k high-poly onto each retopo (Cycles selected-to-active, cage 0.015*max dim, 16px EXTEND), applied to rigged FBX by shared UVs (max UV delta 0 for all four; mouse has the known 0.0039 Y offset). Fixture judgement at native 105/66/47/190 + 4x: badger/fox/mouse = no clear useful detail gain (subtle shading only); boar = visible but mixed fur/shoulder/coat changes, not uniformly better. Normal maps are sharper in the atlas but come with saturated UV-border wedges and flatter/faceted basecolour patches (fragmented Tripo UV packing). First basecolour bake darkened metals (diffuse pass attenuated by metallic): fixed in the allowed 2nd attempt via emit-transfer of raw Base Color. 4k vs 2k (badger, boar): no visible gain at game size -> 2k is enough, 4k stopped. No green-channel inversion. RM: G=rough, B=metal, unjudged because the fixture forces metallic 0 / smooth 0.25. My read: re-baking from the high-poly is NOT worth adopting as is; the 1k->2k detail gap is not what limits the look at 105 px. Maps sit in ~/Desktop/tripo-g0/baked/<name>/ if you want them anyway. Proposal: skip adoption, go to G2(b) cast style consistency via materials only (one shader, per-class metallic/smoothness ranges cloth/leather/metal/fur, shared grading) — note that the fixture currently forces metallic 0 / smoothness 0.25 on every actor, so (b) needs a fixture variant that lets class ranges through and a class map per character (material slots or a mask). Shall I dispatch (b) as designed above, or do you want it scoped differently? Owner has still not answered the G1 look judgement.
</cross-session-message>
- 06:31 [EN] <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/64406.sock" from-name="agent-roguelite-62" from-mode="bypass">
Architect: G2(a) agreed. Don't adopt the re-bake. Commit the bake scripts unwired on feature/art-g2 with the negative result in the message (no gain at game size; UV-border wedges from Tripo's fragmented packing). Keep the maps on the Desktop and don't import them.

G2(b), rescoped. No class masks: per-character segmentation is real work, and Tripo already gives us the class information in its RM map (G=rough, B=metal per texel). So:
 - Fixture variant: stop forcing metallic 0 / smoothness 0.25. Use Tripo's own RM maps through one shared URP Lit-based material.
 - Unify through a small set of per-character DATA parameters, with the same parameter set for everyone. Global remaps: smoothness scale and clamp, metallic scale, plus albedo saturation, contrast and value. Put them where character data already lives (characters.json, or a material preset file referenced from it). No C# literals, per asset-separation.
 - One pass: pick values so that the four characters read as one game. The badger currently reads too glossy; the boar reads too photoreal. Deliver the same lineup sheet (idle 4x, native, silhouettes) as before-vs-after.

Owner gate: I am asking the owner directly in my session right now about the G1 look (accept or not) and about whether the style mismatch between the boar and the badger even matters to them. Wait for my relay before dispatching (b). If the owner says the mismatch doesn't matter, (b) is dropped.
</cross-session-message>

<summary>Agent "Hook falls back to terminal on timeout" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The Claude hook now falls back to the normal terminal prompt when no Feishu answer arrives, instead of denying the tool. The full suite passes (30 tests, `OK`), and the diff touches only the two allowed files.

## Changes Made
- **`agent_channel/claude_hook.py`**
  - **Budget:** `AGENT_CHANNEL_HOOK_TIMEOUT` (default 120) is now a total budget, measured with `time.monotonic()` from the start of `main()`. For each question, `remaining = budget - elapsed`. If `remaining &lt;= 0`, the hook falls back without asking.
  - **Ask command:** it gets `--timeout max(1, ceil(remaining))`, and `subprocess.run` gets `timeout=remaining + 10`.
  - **Fallback:** a new `_ask_terminal()` prints `permissionDecision: "ask"` with a reason and no `updatedInput`. It replaces the old `_deny` calls for exception or timeout, non-zero return code, status other than `answered`, empty answer, and exhausted budget.
  - **Cancel:** a new `_cancel()` runs `[bin, 'cancel', '--request', id]` with `capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10` and swallows all exceptions. It runs before falling back on every path where an ask was started. Questions never asked get no cancel.
  - **Unchanged:** the two `_deny` cases (invalid stdin JSON and no questions), the early `return 0`, the success output, the request-id derivation, and the prompt building.
  - **One addition you didn't ask for:** a non-numeric `AGENT_CHANNEL_HOOK_TIMEOUT` falls back to 120 instead of raising.
- **`tests/test_adapters.py`**
  - `test_channel_failure_returns_explicit_denial` is replaced by `test_channel_failure_falls_back_to_terminal_prompt`, which expects `ask` and no `updatedInput`.
  - New tests, one per case: `test_timed_out_status_falls_back_and_cancels_request` (a), `test_subprocess_timeout_falls_back` (b), `test_empty_answer_falls_back` (c), `test_ask_command_carries_timeout_within_budget` (d), and `test_second_question_timeout_discards_answers_and_cancels_only_second` (e).
  - I added a `run_questions` helper and two small static helpers (`completed`, `cancels`) to support them.

I did not add a test for the exhausted-budget path (`remaining &lt;= 0`); the spec didn't list one.

## Verification
- **Old hook:** I temporarily put the backup `claude_hook.py` back and ran `unittest tests.test_adapters.HookBehavior`. It ran 9 tests with `FAILED (failures=5, errors=1)`:
  - ERROR: `test_ask_command_carries_timeout_within_budget`
  - FAIL: `test_channel_failure_falls_back_to_terminal_prompt`
  - FAIL: `test_empty_answer_falls_back`
  - FAIL: `test_second_question_timeout_discards_answers_and_cancels_only_second`
  - FAIL: `test_subprocess_timeout_falls_back`
  - FAIL: `test_timed_out_status_falls_back_and_cancels_request`
- **Restore:** I put the new `claude_hook.py` back. The unchanged success-path and disabled-hook tests passed against both versions.
- **Full suite:** `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.13 -m unittest discover -s tests -v` printed `Ran 30 tests ... OK`. I ran it twice, before and after the old-hook comparison.
- **Diff:** `diff -ru ... agent-channel.bak-fallback agent-channel --exclude=__pycache__` shows only `claude_hook.py` and `test_adapters.py`.
- **Safety:** all subprocess calls in the tests were mocked, so the real `agent-channel` never ran and nothing was sent to Feishu. I touched nothing under `~/.codex` or `~/.claude`, and the temp copy in `/tmp` is deleted. The backup is kept at `/Users/youxun/.local/share/agent-channel.bak-fallback`.

Files changed:
- `/Users/youxun/.local/share/agent-channel/agent_channel/claude_hook.py`
- `/Users/youxun/.local/share/agent-channel/tests/test_adapters.py`</result>

<result>`claude_hook.py` now names the group after the current Claude Code session, and all 39 tests pass, 9 of them new. I touched only `agent_channel/claude_hook.py` and `tests/test_adapters.py`, and the backup is at `/Users/youxun/.local/share/agent-channel.bak-sessionname`.

## Changes Made
- `agent_channel/claude_hook.py`:
  - Added `_last_transcript_names(path)` and `_session_name(event, session, cwd)`, and added `import glob`. `display_name` is now `_session_name(event, session, cwd)`, computed once before the question loop.
  - Priority is the `AGENT_CHANNEL_SESSION_NAME` env var, then the last non-empty `agentName`, then the last non-empty `aiTitle`, then `&lt;cwd basename&gt; &lt;short_id&gt;` as before.
  - The transcript is read line by line, and only lines containing `"type":"agent-name"` or `"type":"ai-title"` are parsed. Malformed lines are skipped.
  - The path is `transcript_path` if it is a file, otherwise the first match of `~/.claude/projects/*/&lt;session_id&gt;.jsonl`. Any exception falls through to the next priority.
- `tests/test_adapters.py`:
  - Added a `session_name_for` helper that patches `os.path.expanduser` to a temp home, uses a fake session id, and mocks `subprocess.run`.
  - Added 9 tests covering your cases (a) to (g). I split (e) into two tests, one with `transcript_path` missing and one with it pointing at a non-file. I also added an unreadable-transcript test for (f).
  - The existing tests are unchanged.

## Verification
- **New tests against the old `claude_hook.py`:** 6 of the 9 fail. The other 3 pass only because the old code already gives the same result: env wins, the fallback name, and the unreadable transcript:
  - `test_session_name_agent_name_beats_later_ai_title`
  - `test_session_name_ai_title_only_uses_last`
  - `test_session_name_empty_agent_name_falls_through_to_ai_title`
  - `test_session_name_found_via_projects_glob_when_path_missing`
  - `test_session_name_glob_used_when_transcript_path_is_not_a_file`
  - `test_session_name_last_agent_name_wins`
- **New file:** I restored it after that run, and `python3.13 -m unittest discover -s tests -v` gives `Ran 39 tests ... OK`.
- **Backup diff:** `diff -rq` against `.bak-sessionname --exclude=__pycache__` lists only `agent_channel/claude_hook.py` and `tests/test_adapters.py`.
- **Real transcript** (read-only, env var unset): `_session_name` returned `'Codex 飞书 hook 通知'`.
  - An independent `grep -o` on the same file finds no `agent-name` entry.
  - The last `ai-title` is `"aiTitle":"Codex 飞书 hook 通知"`, so the two agree.
- **Cleanup:** I left no temp files. Test runs created `__pycache__` directories under `agent_channel/` and `tests/`; I didn't remove them.</result>
